With the ‘New’ Yeezy, Ye Says He Wants to ‘Let the Clothes Do the Talking.’ But Who’s Listening?

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

“I don’t have a lot of words tonight,” Ye told me, not long after concluding his Yeezy Season 01 runway show in Los Angeles on Wednesday evening. “I’ll let the clothes do the talking.”

You might read that quote and be surprised to hear that Ye said it warmly, with an all-32-bared smile. But for those still paying attention, his relatively upbeat demeanor tracks with the tame-not-turnt persona that Ye has performed through most of 2026; the one that’s been slowly-but-surely trying to reamass the cultural capital he’s spent most of the last decade either annihilating or reclaiming. Legions of fans have proven they’re still all in; the culture at large remains justifiably dubious. The question this week is, how will Ye’s return to fashion affect the calculus?

Upon arrival, a typical Hollywood warehouse exterior turned into a Frank Herbert-esque interior as soon as you crossed the threshold. Inside, a hundred or so guests sat on two rows of unbroken benches that encircled a large white mound in the center, lit by a heavenly white glow from above. Inside was a mix of the usual cool kids: models (including the runway-favorite teen rapper Nettspend) and recurring characters in the overarching, enduring Kanye Universe. There was Fear of God founder Jerry Lorenzo, who made conversation with Don C, while 2 Chainz was patiently posted up in a hooded black leather coat like ’98 Wesley Snipes. Big Sean, rebuffing the sweltering indoor temperatures in a shearling jacket over a crewneck sweater, caught up with Hit-Boy.

Models on the Yeezy Season 01 runway, which featured a characteristically minimalist set piece.

The 19-year-old rapper Nettspend walked in the show.

Once everyone was seated, the lights dimmed as the unmistakable opening piano note of “Runaway” set the show into motion, with one lone model striding out in a killer black trench. From there, two more models, men this time, walked out in the opposite direction; what followed was a bit of organized chaos with the number of models and paths alternating at random intervals to the tune of a playlist that zipped between new Ye music (UK garage vibes, it would seem) and a gothic orchestra not unlike the scores Danny Elfman used to chef up for Tim Burton.

The fits were crisp, practical and tactical. Most of the menswear was ready-made for basic maneuvers, while the women’s looks were sleek and casual, with a few Milla Jovovich-in-Fifth-Element skimpy ensembles thrown in because Ye can only restrain himself but so much. The most notable thing, about the guys especially, were the relatively streamlined silhouettes: close-cropped coats, bombers and vests, fitted pants and sweats. Where past Ye lines championed and emblazoned the path for the baggy wave, with oversized boots and pants you could swim in, Ye seemed to be brazenly planting a flag back on the opposite end of the spectrum.

Nearly 12 years, and a dozen lifetimes, since his debut Yeezy Season 1 runway show in 2015, the notion of a “Season 01”—his first formal showing since his damning 2022 show in Paris, the one with the “White Lives Matter” shirts—is a conspicuous attempt to clear the slate. All of his previous shows had been produced in collaboration with Adidas, who severed ties with Ye four years ago, after he tweeted a tirade of antisemetic remarks. This time around, to let the clothes tell it, Ye is locked in. I don’t have an image of every single Yeezy Season line seared into my brain, but I feel comfortable saying Ye’s latest was the first time I looked at one of his collections and objectively thought, I could wear that. Most of the gear is quite solid—and familiar Ye's running the same playbook with Season 01 that he’s run with his music, and to some degree, his persona. It’s the Yeezy gear you know and love(d), refined to make it that much more palatable, similar to the way a song like “Whatever Works” puts him back over chipmunk’d soul samples. And true to the title, it does indeed; the fact that it does is maybe a testament to the brilliance that got lost in the insanity. But is playing the hits (quite literally, with “Runaway” opening the show) what we want from one of the most paradigm-shifting—and absolutely chaotic—creative minds of our time?

Historically, Ye’s designs are so focused on an idea of utilitarianism that they ultimately come off as a bit drab, if not downright dystopian. Essentials like hoodies and beefy leather jackets are still central to his vision, but as the models strode around in his usual earth-toned palette—save for a handful of black looks to start, and a bright pop of reds at the end—it became clear that Ye was putting on the same show in a different kind of stadium. The same sci-fi silhouettes, tweaked to their cleanest shape and most pleasing hue, sans screenprinted hate speech. Ideologically, the runway show wasn’t far off from the experience of hearing his 2007 hit “Good Life” all these years later, ringing off stadium speakers, carrying 80,000 people along on a wave of wistfulness— even if Ye himself only performed approximately 75% of the lyrics against a backing track.

In keeping with all of his other moves this year, Yeezy Season 01 is Ye presenting a safer, rule-abiding version of himself: Ye with just art, no antics. Much in the way that his latest album, Bully, eschewed any lyrics that say, shouted out R. Kelly or Bill Cosby (which is more than can be said about 2024’s Vultures 1), it felt like a deep sigh of relief to see him present clothes that don’t feature appalling slogans, at a fashion show with Candace Owens nowhere in sight. But the compromise for a sanitized Kanye is also one that can’t help but feel anodyne as well. The New Ye doesn’t needle, but he’s lost Old Ye’s knack for trying something new. Of course, we’re also well over a decade into the Yeezy experiment—at this point, the present has caught up to the futuristic vision Ye first dreamt up back in 2015. There’s a whole new generation of aspiring designers creating in his image, kids who fancy themselves trailblazers whose paths just loop back to Ye: streamer Kai Cenat just launched his own clothing brand, Vivet, while N3on attended Wednesday night’s show, skipping the Yeezy space gleefully documenting every interaction. It feels as though all of this has happened before, and all of it will happen again.

Mystery. Intrigue. Chaos. This is the typical life cycle for a Ye project, be it music, fashion, or otherwise. An announcement materializes, hype ensues. Then, inevitably, the seams start to show and it becomes apparent that he isn’t inviting you on a pre-planned journey so much as building the plane in real time, seconds before takeoff. And then, through sheer force of will, or maybe even genius, the result ends up so captivating that the speedbumps hardly matter. He’s still a master showman, and all it takes is a strong yearning for the Old Ye to find yourself surrendering to it. The efficacy of that last factor, though, has undeniably waned over the years. For much of the 2020s, in the midst of many a manic outburst or worse, Ye’s messes have hardly cohered into something objectively beautiful. The intrigue curdled into perverse bemusement; for every unwavering diehard fan, there was a rubbernecker tapped in to watch the circus.

The 2026 version of Ye has been an exercise in proving how many people will show up when he shuts up, and whether he can make it back from the brink if he delivers the right spectacle.

The result is the most well-oiled the Yeezy Machine has ever been. An album that more or less dropped when he said it would, for once. A smattering of live shows across the world, each in a sold-out stadium boasting record-breaking rap concert attendance where he leans on his most enduring strengths: visual spectacle and a bulletproof catalog. The only area left to renegotiate is fashion…which brings us back to Wednesday night in LA.

These are the scenes you’d expect at a Ye event, but the first sign that the overall vibe was different and more relaxed was that the man himself was at the door, tucked off into a hallway, immaculately dressed in a fitted all-black leather fit offset by a ridiculously large and ornate gold waist fixture—every bit the Dune-caliber fit as the space we were standing in required. Ahead of the show, he posed backstage flanked by his wife, Bianca Censori, and the four kids he shares with his ex, Kim Kardashian (including 13-year-old North, who was sporting her unmistakable blue-raspberry locks). At Yeezy events past, you’d expect to see harried staff scrambling, while Ye was somewhere out of sight but exuding stress and anxiety out into the atmosphere. But on Wednesday, the 49-year-old beamed as friends made their way past him to their seats. Even a three-piece-suited Kid Cudi came over to say hello.

Ye backstage with his children, from left: North, Psalm, Chicago, and Saint West.

Fifteen years ago, Yeezy Season 01 would’ve caused a massive sea change in the menswear world almost instantaneously, with the wave starting in SoHo then slowly emanating out into the beyond. In 2026, does Kanye still move that needle? The Year in Ye has been one big display of his ability to Don Draper the culture at large, and shock us all by “how much it never happened.” There’s no controversy big enough to keep him away for too long and very few fences he can’t mend.

For example, Sean and Cudi ceased their umpteenth falling out with their old label boss two months ago at a guest-packed, blockbuster homecoming show in Chicago on Ye’s tour stop; their show of support tonight only underlined how deeply the hatchet has been buried yet again. When I spoke to Big Sean after the show, he could only marvel at how much Ye has “given” to the culture across all different categories. One-time GOOD Music COO Steven Victor said the show made him feel like Ye “has his shit together,” noting that Ye admitted to him that he was still working out the model patterns hours before showtime. In past Yeezy Seasons, the cram-effect would’ve been apparent, but here, Victor said he wouldn’t have noticed because it all seemed to go off so effortlessly.

As for the man himself? I spoke to him, but again, he didn’t have much to say, at least on the record. He seemed excited that I picked up on the intentional disharmony of the model routes, and explained that the space we were standing in was a “language I’ve been working on for 10 years” called “DROAM,” which refers to the minimalist design he hopes to apply to stores, urban planning, and a home for himself.

“I got a quote for you,” Victor interjected. “The king returns!” Ye laughed. The question remains, though: Is he welcome?

The Yangwang U9 Xtreme Just Set Two Guinness World Speed Records

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

The electric Chinese supercar went more than 312 mph on a public road in the Abu Dhabi desert.

The fastest production car in the world. No, not necessarily an attribute that would be useful in your real life, unless your driveway is a section of unrestricted autobahn, but as an achievement, it's something automakers love to boast about. Porsche. Ferrari. Bugatti. And now Yangwang.

The luxury division of Chinese giant BYD (as Lexus is to Toyota), Yangwang's appropriately named U9 Xtreme hypercar just hit 312 mph on a public road in Abu Dhabi. This feat nets it two Guinness World Records at once: the single-direction highest speed for both a production car and for a production electric car.

At this point, perhaps the electric production speed record doesn't need its own subdivision, especially since the U9 was chiefly breaking its own previous record of 308 mph, set last year. The U9 Xtreme, or U9X, has four electric motors, one at each wheel, for a grand total of over 3000 hp. That's three Veyrons concentrated into a single car.

See all results for used cars for sale near 34472

The battery architecture is also distinct from the standard U9, with a 1200-volt architecture and the ability to both send and receive huge amounts of power with greatly improved thermal efficiency. Even when nearly depleted, at 20 percent charge, it can still pump out 1800 kilowatts of power (2414 hp), or soak up 700 kilowatts through regenerative braking.

A motor at each corner means almost limitless fine-tuning for torque-vectoring control, meting out all that power with minimal wheelslip. The U9X also features electric dampers that clamp down on squat and dive under hard acceleration and braking, and active aerodynamic elements to maintain stability at speed. A tip of the hat also goes to Indonesia's Giti Tires, who developed the rubber capable of putting up with these tremendous speeds and forces.

Yangwang only plans to build thirty of these U9X models, and it's not like you can just slap on a license plate and bully 911 Turbos on the Autobahn. The 4.35-mile stretch of public road used for the record attempt was closed at the time, and also fastidiously cleaned of debris, including going back over the surface looking for any wires left by the street sweepers.

However, as a demonstration of just how advanced BYD's battery chemistry can be, this new speed record represents all kinds of bragging rights. Speed records come and go. For now, nothing can touch the Yangwang U9 Xtreme.

Report: Surprise €10m deal tabled for Liverpool veteran

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Liverpool have a lot of decisions that they need to make on their squad over the next few months.

A handful of their first-team squad all have their contracts expiring in June 2027 and, with no negotiations lined up for any of them, another massive overhaul could be just around the corner.

Of course, the number of transfers that Liverpool have been linked with is obscene but, at least it proves that the Reds are casting their net far and wide for their next transfers.

Some positions are obviously more in need of reinforcement than others but, since Liverpool could end up moving some of their own players on as well, nobody actually knows what the next year or so could look like for the Anfield outfit.

That is, especially when multi-million deals are put up for grabs.

AC Milan want to offer Virgil van Dijk €10 million per year to join them from Liverpool

Virgil van Dijk has easily been one of Liverpool's best players ever since he joined.

No matter who he's been partnered with or who the Reds have faced, the Dutchman has always managed to come out as one of the stand-out performers.

Indeed, things have gone a bit wrong for him every now and then but, more often than not, he's proven why he's one of the world's best centre-backs.

However, now that he's 35 years old, questions are being asked about how long he can keep these performance levels up for.

Liverpool have already started to line up players such as Giovanni Leoni and Jeremy Jacquet to replace him but, right now, Andoni Iraola's men would be lost without their captain in the starting XI.

So, that's why the latest update from MilanNews24 is so surprising.

"According to what MilanNews24 has learned, Cardinale would like to make a concrete attempt," they wrote.

"It's not just a tempting idea or a name linked to AC Milan to fuel the fans' ambitions: the Rossoneri owner is reportedly willing to seriously evaluate the conditions for bringing the Dutchman to Italy.

"To land Van Dijk, there are two key conditions. The first concerns Champions League qualification , a crucial element in presenting the defender with an ambitious and competitive sporting project.

"The second is naturally economic: a two-year contract worth 8-10 million euros per season would be on the table .

"A significant figure, which testifies to the scope of the investment envisioned by Cardinale.

"AC Milan is therefore dreaming of an international signing for the summer of 2027. Van Dijk would be a signing capable of bringing experience, leadership, and personality. Now they need to turn that dream into a concrete possibility."

Virgil van Dijk is still one of Liverpool's most important players

As mentioned, Van Dijk is key to the way that Liverpool play at the moment.

When he's left on the bench for a cup tie or a less important game, the difference in quality is staggering.

The Dutchman may not seem like he does all that much when he's on the pitch but, in reality, he's the glue that holds the team together.

Of course, other players such as Ronald Araujo or Joe Gomez can try and plug the gap left by the Dutchman but, neither of them are anywhere near his level.

It was always going to be a worry that Van Dijk would leave for free at the end of this season but, the fact that a concrete bid is about to come from AC Milan is surprising.

Most reports that emerge from Italy make it clear that most Serie A clubs are skint with several outfits often linked with haggling Liverpool for a discount on Federico Chiesa's impending transfer.

So, the fact that AC Milan are willing to give Van Dijk around €10 million per year is shocking.

His stature and name in the game will obviously carry a lot of weight but, unless you're a team who knows you can afford his wages such as Liverpool, it feels a tad strange for him to be targeted by anyone else.

Dungeons & Dragons Unveils Brand-New Species You Can Play For Free

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Dungeons and Dragons has just officially revealed a brand-new species that you can play for free during your adventures and campaigns, without the need to purchase a book supplement. The TTRPG has just seen the early access release of the World of Warcraft expansion for D&D, a new supplement that adapts elements from Blizzard’s MMORPG. The book comes with new classes, like Death Knight and Demon Hunter. There are also new subclass options in D&D: World of Warcraft, like the Oath of the Holy Paladin and the revised Swashbuckler Rogue. On top of that, the expansion offers 16 playable species.

Despite the fact that a whole lot of content has just been released as part of the World of Warcraft expansion, Wizards of the Coast is still treating players who haven’t necessarily purchased it. As part of the November Drop for D&D Beyond members, several freebies were announced, including new weapon options, monsters to fight, feats to pick, and one new species. All of the new content in the November Drop shared on D&D Beyond is centered around the themes of Ysgard and the Upper Planes, which is why a CR 9 Valkyrie is highlighted as a new monster option.

Free species for D&D players has become something new since September, when Wizards of the Coast made the Duskling free for Beyond members. This time around, however, the species in question is the Bariaur. This centaur-like creature’s type is Celestial, as Bariaurs are either born in Ysgard or have ancestors there. Instead of having the lower body of a horse, however, they have the body of a sheep, while the upper part resembles a humanoid – this can be human, elf, or dwarf, for instance.

The Bariaur has a Medium size and a base Speed of 35 feet. This new species also has Darkvision with a range of 60 feet. Playing a Bariaur gives you proficiency in Animal Handling, Arcana, Nature, or Survival, as part of the Wanderer’s Instinct trait. With Ram, when you move 10 feet or more straight toward a creature and hit it with an Unarmed Strike as part of the Attack action on your turn, you can use both the Damage and Shove options, once per turn. This makes the Bariaur species work well with Monks in Dungeons and Dragons.

The Bariaur also has the Stable Stance trait. With it, the character has Advantage on saving throws to avoid the Prone condition. This is not necessarily a broken new species in D&D like the Dracthyr from the World of Warcraft expansion, but it is certainly a nicely balanced one that gives players the option to not play as an often tiresome Humanoid. As a Medium character, Small characters can even ride them, potentially creating a fun dynamic between two characters.

This is not the first time this species is playable, but it is the first time it can be played in 5.5e. The Bariaur was introduced as a playable race back in 2nd Edition, and last appeared as a character option in 3.5e. It’s a major release that newer players may not even be aware of, and a fun little nod to veterans who have been playing Dungeons and Dragons for decades.

The Video Games And More You Should Play This Weekend – November 6, 2026

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

This week and weekend is all about the remake of The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time, one of the few games I feel deserves the title of masterpiece. And the remake is really well done! I got every heart piece, every Gold Skulltala, the Goron Sword, and the biggest pouch for every one of my items over the course of the review... but I am contemplating jumping right back in for another go.

We also revealed our next cover and in case you missed the announcement it is... pause for effect... Tomb Raider: Legacy of Atlantis! It's an awesome cover that celebrates the history of Lara Croft, and along with a cover story on the game, we also have a big retrospective on the series. I highly recommend subscribing to get your issue if you haven't already.

But it wasn't all just Zelda and Tomb Raider this week. Below you will find a list of weekend play recommendations, but before that... some of our biggest stories of the week.

We have been writing a lot about The Legend Of Zelda: Ocarina Of Time remake this week because it is one of 2026's most exciting games, and it's real good. The remake is not particularly surprising. It mostly sticks to the trajectory of the original game, but there is a reason it is considered one of the best games of all time. It also looks and plays amazing.

You can read the full review here (or watch the video above), but we also have a big interview with the game's producers (one of whom worked on the original Ocarina of Time, and one who directed Breath of the Wild among many other Zelda games), some details about the Easter Eggs that did and did not make it into the remake, and a very handy tip for the game if you happen to be playing with a Switch 2 Pro controller.

World of Warcraft: Forever is out now and it’s a great time to jump into the revamped and expanded version of a project that changed online games forever back in 2004. A thousand new quests, multiple new zones, countless revamps, and an impressively stunning visual overhaul make it the perfect time to jump in for anyone who’s ever been curious to explore Azeroth.

2022's Escape Academy was a sneaky little puzzle game that surprised me and its sequel released this week. Taking place in a school obsessed with puzzles, you play as a student who must make their way through a series of escape rooms. The puzzles are clever, and the setting is charming, but the secret weapon is the local, split-screen co-op. Solving puzzles together is fun especially when one player needs to look at one section of a room to help dictate a solution on the other side of the room. My wife and I both love video games, but rarely play co-op games together just because of our disparate, individual tastes. But Escape Room is one we enjoyed together and we're so far enjoying the sequel. My only complaint so far is... I wish it ran a little better.

While we collectively use the term here for the sake of familiarity, I’m always a bit hesitant in my own mind to label a game under the wide banner of “Metroidvania.” In part, that’s because I think the two referenced games (Metroid and Castlevania: Symphony of the Night) are actually more different than they might appear at first glance. Gloomsoft’s recent release, Moadra, recognizes that distinction, crafting a game that draws heavy inspiration from the specific tradition of Super Metroid, even while presenting an intriguing and atmospheric fiction that is great fun to discover. In movement, actions, and exploration, Moadra feels remarkably similar to  the best of the 2D Metroid games. A scanning tool also leans into the Metroid inspirations, revealing details about the weird alien world you’re navigating. If you’ve been hungry for a game that scratches that particular itch of sci-fi gear/ability-gated exploration, I think you’ll find a lot to love in this game.

I get the Marvel fatigue we've all been experiencing. There are a lot of games and a lot of movies and a lot of TV shows and many are good, but lately most feel like homework. For those reasons, you'd be forgiven for dismissing the 2021 Guardians of the Galaxy game, but I promise you it is good. For one, it is standalone. It does not directly tie to any specific Marvel media. No homework necessary. And for two, it's just a really good game with an affecting story and a unique take on the characters that most of us met for the first time in the movies. And, for the BioWare fans out there mourning the loss of Mass Effect, this game has some Mass Effect energy. It's available on Switch 2 this week and I happily recommend it, especially if you missed it five years ago.

This exclusively two-player board game features a fun mix of activities. You act as both a dungeon architect, and an adventurer delving into said dungeons. Each player creates their own version of a challenging dungeon filled with traps, monsters, and treasures.

But you’re also forced to take on the dangers of your opponent’s crafted dungeon, to see who manages to come out on top. If you have fond memories of the hidden-info nature of old games like Battleship, but you want some greater strategic depth and fantasy flavor thrown in, this one is a great pick.

NBC Quietly Writes Out Three More Chicago Fire Characters After Cruz's Exit

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Warning! This post contains spoilers from Chicago Fire season 15, episode 5, "Three Hundred."After it was revealed that Chicago Fire would lose another original character, all eyes were on their upcoming exit. After 15 seasons, original Chicago Fire cast member Joe Miñoso has said goodbye to Cruz, and it was certainly a bittersweet moment. His exit in the show's 300th episode was meaningful, especially after the previous season's buildup made it seem like Chicago Fire was going to deliver a fate worse than death.

During the emotional turmoil, many didn't stop to think about what his exit would mean for the story as a whole. After all, one character's absence can change everything; relationships and recurring characters connected to the overall story suddenly have no place in the ongoing narrative. Chicago Fire season 15 didn't just say goodbye to Cruz. Three other characters connected to his journey will no longer have a reason to appear.

Cruz isn't simply leaving Firehouse 51. He's leaving Chicago, which means Chloe, Javi, Otis, and the twins are leaving with him. These characters play a crucial role in the series' overall message. How will Chicago Fire deal with their absence? Who will replace their function in the story?

Chicago Fire is no longer a show just about the Firehouse 51 family — it never really has been. They have families outside their careers, too; the longer they stick around, the more they become part of the show's DNA. Cruz's family — Chloe, Javi, and young Otis — has been a solid part of the series for the last eight years. Chloe first arrived in Chicago Fire season 7. Since her introduction, Cruz has matured and gained a family that he loves dearly.

Now that Cruz is gone, audiences won't get to see his family anymore. During the 300th hour of Chicago Fire, Cruz made it clear that the family was moving to Minnesota. Chloe's parents are there to help with the twins, and the rehab center he needs is close to their house. He'll still be involved, and Chloe can work remotely. There's no easy way to bring any of the characters back, especially not without Cruz, as they have no real connection to the rest of the Firehouse 51 family.

During his nearly 15-season tenure, Cruz has grown from a somewhat directionless firefighter trying to support his brother into a family man and an inspiration to other characters. Out of all the original characters who could have been written out during Chicago Fire season 15, he'll leave the biggest hole in the show. In an interview with TV Insider, Miñoso explained the reasoning behind his character's exit:

"It was hard to hear at first, but at the same time, I really understood it. When you spend this much time, there are some firefighters that get maybe 20 years out of a career, and I’m five short of that. So I feel like I might as well have gone through the actual service... The fact that we got to tell such a well-rounded story for one individual, and as a Latino man, to be able to tell such a positive story about a Latino male and a Latino family and what we bring to this community, I think that as shocking as it was, I understood it and I’m just proud that we got to tell the stories we did."

From a narrative perspective, Chicago Fire delivered a beautiful, meaningful exit. Cruz chose his family over his career. Though he'd love nothing more than to return to Squad, he finally saw the one thing staring him in the face: fear. His family was terrified that he would go back to work and the next call would be his last. He saw their disappointment; it almost looked like he was choosing strangers over them.

In the end, he realized that his family needed him far more than anyone else in Chicago. He'll continue his rehab, motivated to walk again for Chloe and the children, rather than for his own personal goals. While Chicago Fire season 1-era Cruz would have balked at the idea of walking away and may have even been terrified about this next chapter, season 15-era Cruz is more certain of what life has in store for him and what he has to offer.

Losing Cruz's family, in addition to the original character, is disappointing, especially for those who watch the show for more than the dangerous, adrenaline-fueled calls. Chloe offered another insight into what it is like to be a firefighter's spouse. While Cindy and Trudy are in a similar position in their relationships with Herrmann and Mouch, Chloe is a younger wife just starting to build her family. This is a different and essential perspective.

Cindy was first introduced during a scary incident with Herrmann during Chicago Fire season 1. Despite the risks of Cruz's job and Cindy's own experiences, the show always depicted her as a supportive, understanding spouse. She knows that this is her husband's passion, and she hasn't asked him to leave for the family.

Trudy married Mouch because they're further along in life, and she has her own dangerous job. Through their marriage, Chicago Fire and Chicago PD both showcase what it's like to have two first responders in the house. Chloe's experience is different; she even considered leaving Cruz when they were dating because she was afraid of losing him. Cruz was also a member of Squad 3 and often tackled more dangerous, intense situations, especially compared to Herrmann and Mouch. Cruz's wife was the way to showcase that anxiety and fear, as she tried to hold it all back for the sake of everyone around her, reflecting people's real-life experiences.

Joe Minoso is leaving Chicago Fire as Cruz in season 15, and the latest episode revealed his replacement is already part of the Firehouse 51 crew.

Javi and Otis brought their own arcs, especially Javi. Chicago Fire was able to show a successful adoption and offer a look at how it's okay to want to know more about your birth family, but understand your adoptive family is still there for you. Otis provided an innocent, childlike view of the world, learning what it means to be a firefighter and showing what it's like to grow up worrying that your dad may not make it home from work. In fact, "Three Hundred" included a moving moment for Otis when he asked why "somebody else's daddy" couldn't be part of Squad 3 instead.

Each of these three characters gave the show a different but no less important perspective, one Chicago Fire hasn't explored much over the years. In a way, some of their potential was wasted because they had relatively little screen time and their personal lives weren't always the focus, but they became a crucial part of Cruz's story and the show's world-building. Their relatable, emotional experiences are now missing.

New episodes of Chicago Fire season 15 air Wednesdays at 9 p.m. ET on NBC and the next day on Peacock.

Chicago Fire follows the lives of firefighters, rescue squad members, and paramedics of Firehouse 51 in Chicago as they face professional and personal challenges, showcasing their dedication, teamwork, and resilience.

Stephen Miller named by Trump as new White House chief of staff

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Trump said he would be replacing Susie Wiles for the top advisory role. Miller is controversial figure, known as a key architect of the administration's hardline immigration policies.

US President Donald ‌Trump announced on ⁠Friday ⁠that his ​deputy chief of ​staff and architect of his administration's strict immigration policy, Stephen Miller, will become his new chief of staff.

Miller replaces Susie Wiles, who had been in the post since Trump's inauguration. With the nod, Miller becomes the top aide to the president.

Trump announced the move, saying Miller was "one of my most trusted advisors," adding that Wiles has done an outstanding job, and would be "moving over" to the "Trump political operation."

"He gets it" and is "very good politically. And he's a very good man. He'll do a great job, great manager," Trump said.

Wiles also commented on her exit from the White House, saying that her work there was "the honor of a lifetime."

The move comes after Trump's Republican Party lost control of the House and Senate after a midterm election rout at the hands of Democrats.

The 41-year-old is one of Trump's longest-serving advisers, having joined Trump's longshot 2016 presidential campaign as a speechwriter.

Although his portfolio as deputy chief of staff was wide-ranging, Miller is most responsible for Trump's policies and rhetoric on immigration.

Miller's appointment is a signal that Trump intends to continue on with his hardline immigration policies, despite voters rejecting Republicans around the country.

Miller is known for taking controversial positions, and is one of the Trump administration's most vocal advocates, frequently making media appearances to defend extreme positions.

On immigration, his ideas have included calling for federal agents to make 3,000 arrests every day, while allowing the agency to target migrants who have lived in the US for decades. He is widely created with helping expand the remit of Immigration and Customs Enforcement (ICE).

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

On foreign policy, Miller has defended extrajudicial strikes on boats in Caribbean Sea allegedly used by drugs smugglers, and defended the administration's military operation to snatch Venezuelan leader Nicolas Maduro in January.

In January 2026, Miller went on broadcaster CNN and claimed that the US had the right the seize Greenland, after Trump said controlling the Danish territory was a strategic necessity.

"We live in the real world, Jake, that is governed by strength, that is governed by force, that is governed by power. These are the iron laws of the world since the beginning of time," Miller told CNN's host Jake Tapper. Miller also has not held back on attacking the administration's opponents.

During the midterm campaign, Miller called Democrats "extremists, freaks and America-hating lunatics" on social media.

He also promoted claims about election fraud in California and Alaska, which were not substantiated.

As Trump has lost control of both chambers of Congress, Miller has been credited with working in the background to create an obstructionist strategy for the White House to block Democratic legislation. Trump has taken to the strategy and has said, "I guess I'll become Mr. Veto."

After Trump's announcement, New York Democratic Congressman Hakeem Jeffries, who could become the next speaker of the House, posted on social media that Miller was a "dangerous xenophobe and reckless extremist who has American blood on his hands," referring to the deaths of US citizens protesting ICE raids.

Senator Chuck Schumer issued a statement saying the Trump had "learned nothing" after the Republican's election drubbing.

"Stephen Miller is the architect of the most barbaric, inhumane policies of this administration," said Schumer, who is positioned to become the next Senate majority leader in January.

"Trump couldn't have made a worse choice. Our Democratic majorities will fight him every step of the way," he said.

Earth's churning mantle may have created the Great Unconformity

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Explaining the Great Unconformity, where Cambrian deposits sit on top of older rocks.

In the Grand Canyon, there is a line in the rock where time seems to have gone missing. Ancient crystalline rocks mark its bottom, while sandstones laid down as life started to diversify during the Cambrian explosion mark the top. “It’s called the Great Unconformity, and it’s a gap in the geologic record,” says Nicolas Flament, a geoscientist at the University of Wollongong, Australia. “This gap can be anywhere between 200 million years, which is not that impressive, to more than a billion years.”

In a recent Nature study, Flament and his colleague Santosh Dhungana argue that this gap was carved with help from deep within the planet. According to their models, slow currents of hot rock in Earth’s mantle pushed entire continents up for hundreds of millions of years, letting glaciers grind them down and then let them sink, allowing fresh sediments to bury the scar. The largest hole in the rock record might be a fingerprint of processes happening deep in the planet’s interior.

The oldest sediments resting on the Great Unconformity in North America are between 635 and 482 million years old. Geologists have traced it across most of North America and parts of northern Europe and northern China. “Something seems to have happened, in particular in North America,” Flament says.

The leading explanation was glaciation. Between roughly 720 and 635 million years ago, glaciers reached the tropics, and some scientists think the entire planet froze over, a scenario called Snowball Earth. “Ice sheets are very good at grinding, scouring, scraping the surface,” Flament explains. He said that glaciers are about 10 times more effective than rivers at eroding rock away. But if the whole planet froze, the Great Unconformity should be found everywhere. And it’s not.

Other explanations included rifting, pulling and stretching of Earth’s crust, and a global drop in sea levels. But rifting acts mostly on the edges of continents, and a sea level change should have affected all continents alike. “There’s no doubt that there was extensive glaciation. There’s no doubt that there have been sea-level changes throughout time. The question is, was that enough to put the Great Unconformity puzzle together?” Flament says.

A fourth idea held that the mantle itself was moving continents up and down, a process called dynamic topography. “It had been proposed, but it hadn’t been quantitatively tested,” Flament says. So, his team set out to do so.

From roughly 100 to 200 kilometers beneath the surface down to nearly 3,000 kilometers, Earth’s mantle slowly convects. “An analogy would be a lava lamp, where some stuff comes up and some stuff comes down,” Flament explains. “Cold rock sinks and hot rock rises.”

The mantle is solid, yet a piece of rock can slowly travel from the surface to the deep Earth over some 200 million years. That motion tugs on the surface. Where cold slabs of ocean floor sink into the mantle at subduction zones, the ground gets pulled down; where hot mantle rises, it gets pushed up. The amplitude of this up and down motion is still debated, though. “The short of it is several hundreds of meters, and it’s still to be worked out exactly what that [precise value] is,” Flament says.

Dhungana and Flament ran six simulations of mantle convection going back 1 billion years, driven by reconstructions of how tectonic plates moved over that time. Then they grouped regions where the ground rose and fell in similar ways to find where the unconformity should have formed, and where it should have survived to the present. Flament, however, says the starting configuration of these simulations remains uncertain.

Magnetic records in ancient rocks reveal how far continents were from the equator, but not their longitude. “It’s possible that some continents may be in the wrong hemisphere,” Flament admits. The ancient ocean floor, long since recycled into the mantle, had to be invented entirely from scratch in the models. Still, the simulations produced a consistent story.

Laurentia, the ancient core of North America, had little or no subduction along its margins between about 930 and 510 million years ago, much like Africa today, so it sat above some rising mantle and was pushed up. Five of the six models showed this pattern of Laurentia seeing long uplift followed by sinking in the Cambrian. For northern Europe and North China, this pattern showed up in four.

But the uplift alone, Flament’s team concluded, was too gentle to strip away enough rock to create the Great Unconformity.

“The uplift could create a tilt for the whole continent, but it’s not great at creating a steep slope,” Flament says. The missing piece of the puzzle, he argues, were glaciers that ground through rock even on low ground and gentle slopes. The models show the uplift was still building when Snowball Earth hit. “This gentle uplift that’s continuous, and then that glacial erosion that comes on top, work together,” Flament explains. “They’re not coupled. They just happen to coincide in time.” When the continents sank in the Cambrian, seas flooded in and buried the eroded surface.

The models made by Flament’s team also explain why the gap is so obvious in North America but hard to find elsewhere. Compared with records of sediment preserved on each continent, North America and Eurasia show one long rise followed by one long fall, which the researchers interpret as the ideal recipe for the Great Unconformity.

South America and Australia, on the other hand, went through two cycles. “This would form potentially two unconformities, and then the Great Unconformity would be more difficult to see,” Flament says. Africa was a different case altogether—hemmed in by mountain belts at the time, its erosion and sediment were shaped by the mountains rather than by the mantle beneath.

But if the mantle goes through such up and down cycles on a regular basis, the remaining question was why we can see only one Great Unconformity, and not many.

The team says the Great Unconformity is unique in the geological record because of a combination of a rise-and-fall cycle lasting over 900 million years, a near-global glaciation that happened at the right moment, and rock records that get harder to read farther back in time.

Flament suspects there may be an older candidate, tied to a glaciation 2.4 billion years ago, but rocks that old lack the fossils that make younger sediments easier to date. “Having life in sediments makes it a lot easier to have a sense of time,” he says. His work, however, may help explain what happens right after the Great Unconformity formed.

The intense erosion that carved the Great Unconformity washed rock material into the oceans, possibly fertilizing them just before the Cambrian explosion, when calcium became abundant in seawater. “That’s when life learns to mobilize calcium and create skeletons and shells,” Flament says. He admits, though, that mantle dynamics likely wasn’t the only process that enabled the Cambrian explosion. “I think it would be misleading to say the mantle set the pace here. But I think it was one of the forces that worked together.”

Nature, 2026.  DOI: 10.1038/s41586-026-11156-5

Post-Trail Blues: Learning to Live Off the Trail

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

I miss the trail. It went by too fast. Where did the time go? Sometimes, I feel like I must have blacked out somewhere in the middle because suddenly, I was in New England. It’s hard to wrap my head around the fact that I did something so incredible, so challenging, and so far beyond what I ever thought I was capable of.

I would do anything to be back out there.

Smiling somewhere in a fire tower in New Jersey.

I never understood the people who thru-hike the Appalachian Trail multiple times. But the other morning, the thought crossed my mind, and for the first time, I understood the desire. No, I don’t think I would ever actually hike the entire trail again. But to quiet the mental battles I’m facing right now? To find that peace again? I would do it in a heartbeat.

I miss my friends. I miss who I was out there. I miss the simplicity of it all, the freedom of not constantly stressing about the future or wondering how I’m going to remain happy, fulfilled, and inspired. Out there, I didn’t have to have everything figured out. I just had to keep moving forward, one day and one mile at a time.

Coming back to the real world has been harder than I expected. People don’t look at me the same way, or maybe I’m just seeing things differently now. I don’t know if it’s envy, indifference, or if people are simply caught up in their own battles. Some people have no idea what I just accomplished, and others can’t seem to wrap their heads around it.

I’d be lying if I said I wasn’t disappointed. I wish more people asked me about my hike. I wish more people were curious about what I experienced, what I learned, and what it took to get through it. I wish more of the people who said they would join me had shown up along the way.

Words of encouragment outside of Hanover, NH

I think a part of me expected to come home and have people want to hear every little detail, to understand just how much this meant to me.

But I also know that life doesn’t revolve around me, my experiences, or my wants and needs. Everyone has their own lives to live and their own battles to fight. Just because this experience changed me doesn’t mean everyone else will know how to understand it.

I talked about these feelings with other thru-hikers who were experiencing something similar.  A few mentioned that this was one of the reasons they blogged. Writing gave them an outlet for sharing everything they’d experienced, a place to tell their stories and get all those thoughts out of their heads. Hopefully, those stories find an audience who cares, but even if they don’t, there’s something valuable about having a place to put it all.

That really resonated with me and inspired me to start writing for The Trek again. Maybe this is another way to stay connected to the trail, revisit those memories, and share a little piece of the experience with others.

And now, here I am, trying to figure out what comes next.

What am I going to do with my life? How do I hold on to the person I was out there? How do I find that same sense of purpose, peace, and inspiration when I’m back in a world that feels so much louder and more complicated?

Looking for moose on Ethan Pond in New Hampshire

And perhaps the question that keeps creeping into my mind: How soon can I hike another long-distance trail?

I knew coming home would be hard, but I don’t think I fully understood just how much I would miss the trail until I was no longer on it. I thought finishing would feel like an ending worth celebrating. Instead, it feels like I left a part of myself out there somewhere, and I’m still trying to figure out how to bring it home.

Hey! I’m Alex, known on trail as the Bird Detective. This March, I’m hiking NOBO on the Appalachian Trail. Born a Hoosier and now proudly calling Vermont home, I spend my off-trail days designing socks at Darn Tough Vermont. You’ll usually find me outside, chasing miles or identifying birds by sight, song, and attitude. I’ve completed the Indiana “Hoosier” Triple Crown, the Long Path, Northville–Placid Trail, and the Wonderland Trail. I'm also in the process of piecing together the Cohos Trail, Long Trail Side-to-Side, and NH48.

‘Everyone knows why we lost,’ JD Vance points to the Iran war — which has already cost at least $38 billion and 19 American lives

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

While votes were still being counted in the U.S. midterm elections, administration figures in Washington were already inviting speculation over how the results might be connected to the conflict with Iran.

On the morning after the election, Vice President JD Vance wrote on X that the major reason for the dramatic loss for Republicans was the American public’s opposition to the war. “Everyone knows why we lost. And any honest conversation about where we go from here must start there,” he wrote, in reference to the ongoing conflict that began with U.S.-Israeli airstrikes against Iran in late February 2026.

While it is rare for a vice president to make such a critical proclamation about their own administration’s policy, President Donald Trump later stated that he did not disagree with Vance’s appraisal of the role that frustration with the Iran war played in voters’ minds.

But whether this changes Trump’s view on next steps in the ongoing conflict remains unclear. Reporting over the past few weeks has indicated that the U.S. military has been actively preparing options and increasing capabilities in the Persian Gulf region in the event the president directs a resumption of hostilities.

As an expert on Middle East affairs and a former senior official at the National Security Council during the first Trump administration, I’m aware that months of conflict have failed to bring Iran to its knees, force regime change or militarily defeat a perceived threat to U.S. assets and allies. Escalating now is unlikely to change those realities – and it carries the additional risk of further damaging Republicans in the eyes of Americans voters as they turn toward the 2028 presidential race.

Even if Trump were to ratchet up the conflict, moreover, it’s unclear how much wiggle room he’ll have come January facing a Democratic Congress almost uniformly opposed to the war.

Part of the issue facing Iran war hawks in Washington now is that the conflict has caused serious setbacks for the regime in Tehran without satisfying the officially stated strategic reasons for going to war.

They argue that pulling back now – as welcome as that might be for Americans struggling with the associated rising cost of fuel – may allow Iran to rebuild before the conflict is “won.” Moreover, it would render the military and reputational costs already sunk by Washington as wasted.

Among the achievements of this war touted by the Trump administration has been the death of former Supreme Leader Ali Khameini, along with the killing of scores of senior leaders within the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps – including individuals like the former head of Iran’s National Security Council Ali Larijani. Significant elements of Iran’s air defense and radar network, ballistic missile and drone production, and storage and launch sites have also been destroyed. And Iran’s already outdated navy and air force has been further degraded.

Meanwhile, the U.S.-imposed naval blockade against Iranian-linked shipping in the Persian Gulf cut off a crucial lifeline for Iran’s economy. And additional sanctions and economic penalties imposed by Washington have served to further devalue Iran’s currency and deprive Iran of additional financial links to the international banking system.

Yet for all the U.S. successes in degrading the Iranian military by limitless aerial bombardment and the significant financial pressure against the Iranian economy, an obvious endgame remains elusive.

Despite weakening the Iranian government – now led by Ali Khameinei’s son, Mojtaba, and a new echelon of senior IRGC commanders – the Islamic Republic remains intact. Moreover, it still possesses enough ballistic missiles and drones to hold the region at risk, while continuing to impose significant control over its population.

Meanwhile, U.S. military spending continues to rise. In September, the U.S. Congressional Budget Office assessed that the military costs of the Iran War had already totaled US$38 billion. Since then, that number has only increased. And that is to say nothing of the associated global economic costs that have resulted from Iran’s de facto closure of the Strait of Hormuz – a key shipping route for oil and gas.

Should Trump decide to change course on Iran, he – and others – may think that the money, effort and lives of 19 American servicemen killed in the conflict would have been spent with no obvious objective achieved. Yet continued military expenditure with no strategic endgame could only magnify those same costs.

To Iran, the midterm results have not changed its public position. Mojtaba Khameini has issued statements directing the IRGC to widen the conflict in the region and perhaps beyond should the U.S. and Israel attack again. At the same time, a statement attributed to him indicates that hostilities between the United States and Iran will persist regardless of the balance of party political power in Washington.

This raises questions about the prospects for diplomacy. During the summer, the U.S. and Iran agreed to an initial 14-point memorandum of understanding that, if finalized, could have brought a permanent end to the hostilities. But since the 60-day clock on that agreement lapsed, no parallel diplomatic agreement has emerged.

Even with the major shift in the U.S. political landscape, it remains unclear whether Trump will resume operations against Iran before the new Congress is sworn in. The messy election result in Israel – which has left the pro-war, and potentially outgoing, Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyanu with potentially more reason to double-down in Iran – only complicates matters.

The U.S. military certainly appears to be ready to engage in a new round of attacks against Iran if so directed, and Iran is ready to retaliate. But given the shortcomings in the U.S. inventory – with munitions and missile and air defense capabilities depleted by eight months of war – escalation is something Trump and his inner circle will have to consider with caution.

Iran might force the issue by engaging in asymmetric attacks in the region or beyond that they have not attempted already. Or it could launch a coordinated offensive with its partners in the region – notably the Houthis in Yemen, who have become more active in their own conflict with Saudi Arabia in recent months.

But save an event that the Trump administration perceives is giving it cover to act, there are no clear indications emerging from Washington as to which way it will move.

Vance diagnosed a widely perceived political problem for Republicans: Voters don’t want more war. But what he noticeably didn’t mention was a practical solution. The Middle East conflict – and the United States’ role in it – are, as such, locked in a stalemate.

America and Iran have both borne significant political, military and economic costs. A decision not to pursue an endgame would leave those sunk; doing so may only increase them.

Javed Ali, Associate Professor of Practice of Public Policy, University of Michigan

Thomas Tuchel admits his relationship with Jude Bellingham is 'up and down' - but on the mend - as he reveals he's apologised to Kobbie Mainoo who is struggling at 'off a cliff' Man United

📅 2026-11-06  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Published: 14:00 EDT, 6 November 2026 | Updated: 15:27 EDT, 6 November 2026

Thomas Tuchel says his rocky relationship with Jude Bellingham is on the mend after revealing he called his brother Jobe in to the England squad after some cajoling from the Real Madrid star.

Tuchel and Bellingham Snr have not always seen eye to eye during the German's time as England boss.

But Tuchel said today he hopes those days are behind him after he called 21-year-old Borussia Dortmund midfielder Jobe into this squad for next week's Nations League games against Croatia and Spain.

'It was a bit of an up and down [relationship] of course but we are in a good place,' said Tuchel.

'I love to be his coach. He's a top player, a very influential player and I think it goes both ways. If Jude was not feeling comfortable he would not perform on that level.

'In the moment you see he's happy to come into camp, he's happy with the role he has, he's fully invested and now to have the chance to have both of them is just the next step.

Thomas Tuchel has admitted he has an up and down relationship with Jude Bellingham

'I hope we have finished with the down. You never know as a coach!'

With injury to Bournemouth's Alex Scott and Arsenal's Myles Lewis-Skelly suspended for Thursday's Croatia game at Wembley, Tuchel sprung a surprise by elevating Jobe Bellingham from the Under 21s to the senior squad.

'Yeah Jude and I have spoken about his brother because I was aware it was always his dream to play with Jobe for England,' explained Tuchel.

'Jude was always saying: "Watch him, watch him he's good, he can make it". 'I would say: "Yeah we are watching him".

'I think he actually watched training very early in my reign. He came over and watched his brother train. We chatted and said it would be nice if one day he was with him.

'He was very kind and very polite and I'm glad to have him in camp. Everyone will compare them and he has to live with this.

Now Tuchel says he 'loves' to be Bellingham's coach after his brilliant World Cup campaign

Jude had a hand in brother Jobe being called up to the England squad for the first time

'They look similar, even in their movements, but they are very different players. Jobe is more defensive. I think they are also different characters, at least that's what I heard.

'He couldn't stop smiling when I called to tell him the news. He has the same beautiful smile like Jude so it's nice to have him.'

Tuchel's squad for what he insists are two important games was once again full of messages and insights into this thinking.

There was no room for Cole Palmer and Kobbie Mainoo after both players withdrew from the October campo with injuries. There was also no call up once again for Manchester City's Phil Foden.

Asked about Palmer, fit and ready now and in decent form at Chelsea, Tuchel said: 'It's not personal and we will never assume that he is not desperate to come and play.

'But the competition is on. It's about the ones who walk the walk and come to camp.

'Someone more intelligent once said to me: 'You cannot not communicate. You cannot not send a message.'

'So yeah. A nomination is always a message, yes.'

Tuchel revealed that he almost picked Foden – who hasn't played for England since March – after an upturn in his form but had second thoughts when he saw him relegated back to the bench at the Etihad Stadium.

'He is close and he will always be close,' said Tuchel.

'There were glimpses of the Phil Foden that we would love to see. He had these two matches with four or five goal involvements and assists and being so clinical.

Cole Palmer continues to be left out by Tuchel - while Phil Foden was close to a recall

'I got excited and thought: "That's it, that's, he found his mojo, he is getting back there". Then he dropped again and played again from the bench.

'I'm in love with him as a player. What is not to love?

'If you see the boy train it's just the highest, highest quality.

'But we're talking about numbers in an England shirt which are not in proportion to his talent and to his potential.

'If we can align this and become a regular City starter and be decisive, I think he has to be always an option.

'It was very close and it's also close for Eberechi Eze to keep his place with us. He's not starting for Arsenal.

'But the advantage was maybe that Morgan Gibbs-White and Eberechi Eze come again into camp. The advantage was that they have been in camp last time.'

Thomas Tuchel has told how he apologised in front of the whole England squad to Kobbie Mainoo for not giving him a single minute during the summer's World Cup in America.

Mainoo cut a lost and frustrated figure during England's campaign after a succession of players were picked ahead of him in his central midfield position.

The Manchester United star has not made Tuchel's squad for the forthcoming Nations League games with Croatia and Spain after withdrawing from the last camp with injury.

Tuchel revealed: 'He was hugely disappointed from the World Cup, and deservedly so.

'I said it in front of the team, in front of the staff.

'I said: "Sorry that I didn't pick you, you did nothing wrong, that is on me".

'He was disappointed and I think it cost him a bit of confidence.

A Costa Rican Beach Resort with a World-Class Coffee Program

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

In Hotels With Great Taste, we’re pulling back the curtain for a peek at the “special sauce” that hotels use to create memorable, meaningful culinary experiences for their guests.

Check-in at Nekajui on Costa Rica’s verdant Papagayo Peninsula happens inside a sparsely furnished bungalow the staff call La Casita. But walk across the drive, past a massive ceiba tree hung heavy with lanterns, and all humility vanishes. The whole property sweeps out before you: an endless green lawn, pools cascading down two levels, and, finally, the serenely sparkling bay beyond.

Nekajui is Ritz Carlton’s first Reserve resort in Central or South America. The Reserve’s are Ritz Carlton’s nod to the idea of complete escape, often in the far flung retreats—the Chinese Himalayas, or the Saudi Arabian desert.

Now, the recently opened Nekajui allows you to unwind on the lush Costa Rican coast teeming with wildlife like capuchins, coatis, and iguanas. Hidden away at the remote property, you’ll also find a local coffee program that’s on par with the best big city cafes in the world.

Sprawling over 1400 acres, Nekajui is a dreamy jungle beach resort that opened its doors on the secluded peninsula in February 2025.

Each of the 107 rooms and suites peeks out onto treetop and ocean sunset views. Inside, the decor features outdoor access and carved wood motifs typical of the Guanacaste region.

Many rooms also have access to patios large enough for morning yoga sessions and private plunge pools (take note: if a guest above you has one, you may see water splashing down your way). For larger parties, treetop tents and villas on the property afford even more space than standard rooms.

The main campus includes rooms, restaurants, and a spa, but the property’s grounds extend: a funicular (like a tiny cable car) runs dramatically down the cliffs, past howler monkeys, to the beach with water warm as a pleasant bath and a cave to explore.

Peruvian chef Diego Muñoz, whose career includes stints at world-renowned Spanish restaurants El Bulli and Mugaritz, leads the culinary program at the property’s main restaurants Puna and the tiny, six-seat chef’s counter within, Paruro.

At Puna and Parura, Muñoz’s menu is filled with dishes with the layered flavors and artistic compositions that helped push Peruvian cuisine into the spotlight on a global stage. Puna has a fusion feel , sliding flavors and spices from Munoz’s international travels into Peruvian classics like a robust sudado, and zippy ceviche. Paruro, the chefs counter located inside Puna, is an East Asian experience with bites like a nori-cured tenderloin and a seabass curry. You’ll find one of Munoz’s best creations, a deboned, prawn-stuffed chicken wing, on both menus.

It’s important to Muñoz that Puna uses local ingredients that are uncommon on Costa Rican menus. Fresh seafood, for example, can be a particular challenge to source, even as the overwhelming majority of Costa Rican territory is marine.

Aspirations are high for the beverage program as well. Cooper’s is a moody, mysterious cocktail space with a menu full of unexpected Guanacaste ingredients. It leans into the speakeasy style, and as such, is invite-only, but don’t be intimidated—schmooze your server a bit, or ask your personal concierge to score you an invite.

Inside, the menu is ambitious, dotted with some adventurous local distillates—goat cheese, cascara, mango leaf—which find their way into thrilling riffs on classics like the martini-esque Turrialba using fino sherry and the aforementioned goat cheese distillate.

Elsewhere on the property, you can dine at Niri Beach Club, with an Iberian-focused menu, and the all-day Cafe Rincon which has sandwiches, bowls, and pastries.

Nekajui’s commitment to on-the-ground ingredients shines brightest in its coffee program atCafé Rincon. Fabi Rojas operates the micro-roastery at Nekajui, which features always updating selections from each of Costa Rica’s seven coffee growing regions.

For a country about the size of West Virginia, Costa Rica produces an impressive diversity of coffee: easy-drinking crowd pleasers in the lower elevations of the coastal west, bright acidic pops from Valle Occidental in the north, fruity, prize-winning geishas—one of which fetched over $300 per pound at auction—in the mountainous Los Santos region of the south central. And Fabi has nurtured personal relationships with farmers all over the country to ensure she has access to all of it.

And while she’ll make your coffee however you want it, the move is to have it brewed in a vandola. The one piece clay carafe and pour-over cone looks like something that might date back hundreds of years, but the design actually came from Costa Rican coffee roaster and barista Minor Alfaro in 2015. Brewing in clay helps maintain aroma longer, and the valve into the base of the dripper allows more gentle oxygenation according to Alfaro. Oxygen is usually the enemy of coffee, but allowing a small amount in through the valve can sweeten the flavor.

Besides brewing coffee better than my Chemex, each vandola is a handmade work of ceramic art—an apt vessel to showcase coffees grown, roasted, and brewed with exquisite care.

Thomas Tuchel makes Noni Madueke decision as five Arsenal stars earn England call-ups

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Noni Madueke has missed another England opportunity, despite making an encouraging impression during his recent run in the Arsenal starting line-up.

Thomas Tuchel has named his 25-man squad for the Nations League fixtures against Croatia and Spain in November, but Arsenal winger Madueke misses out again after failing to make the cut following a missed international camp.

He has been left out despite making the most of Bukayo Saka’s absence to increase his involvement under Mikel Arteta.

Madueke scored in the recent Premier League win against Nottingham Forest and then again in Wednesday’s Champions League win against Slavia Prague.

He had a legitimate claim to be named in the squad, and Tuchel admitted that Madueke was very close to being included.

The England boss acknowledged that Madueke has benefitted from his extra games at Arsenal due to Saka’s injury.

But Tuchel chose to keep the same front three that he had during the last international camp. The German wants to reward those players who have earned their place in the team and not make changes every time they are playing well at club level.

This means Madueke will be left frustrated, having to wait until next time.

Madueke has played under Tuchel before, so it shows how much he thinks of him as a player. He featured in England’s 2026 World Cup campaign, so it is noticeable that he was left out this time.

Although he can’t control the battle for places, he will now be able to continue building his case. With Saka still injured, he will have the chance to play more regularly and solidify his place in Arteta’s plans.

However, Madueke is not the only player at Arsenal who has had positive news.

Declan Rice, Eberechi Eze, Ezri Konsa and Myles Lewis-Skelly have all earned or retained their spots in Tuchel’s squad. Ben White also comes back into the England squad, having missed the last camp.

It is another chance for White to re-establish himself on the world stage after his knee injury kept him out of the World Cup.

It also means Arsenal have five players in the squad after Saka was left out due to injury.

England play Croatia at Wembley on November 12 before heading to Spain on November 15. Madueke will be watching from home, knowing he was almost part of the squad.

Now he needs to build on his recent form and become a consistent performer for Arsenal. Whilst Tuchel has left the door open, Madueke needs to give the England boss another reason to change his mind.

Apple has a secret list of banned autocomplete phrases including ‘dictator Trump’ and ‘covid is a hoax’

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

iOS’s keyboard won’t suggest or swipe-type a lengthy list of insults and political phrases.﻿

iOS’s keyboard won’t suggest or swipe-type a lengthy list of insults and political phrases.﻿

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

A researcher found over 100,000 words and phrases in “every major language” that are blocked from autocomplete and swipe-type in iOS, Reuters reports. The researcher, Derik Kauffman, says you can still type the words on the blocklist one letter at a time. But they won’t pop up as predictive text suggestions and iOS’s keyboard may automatically alter them when swipe-typing, like changing “left wing conspiracy” to “left wrong conspiracy.”

Kauffman posted the full list online, including the individual lists for different languages.

Most of the words and phrases on the list are offensive, slurs, or related to topics like sex and drugs. But it also contains political phrases like “illegal immigrant,” “dictator Trump,” “sleepy Joe,” “covid was a hoax,” and “left wing conspiracy” as well as some less controversial phrases like “Santa isn’t real” and “iMessage isn’t working.” Kauffman found some discrepancies on the list, too, where one phrase is blocked while its counterpart isn’t, such as blocking “bad girl” but not “bad boy.”

According to Reuters, Kauffman used OpenAI’s Codex and Anthropic’s Claude Code to help find the list “deep in the bowels” of iOS. On his website, Kauffman says the phrase list was stored as a hash table, which he was able to convert back to text. Another researcher told Reuters they were able to independently obtain the same list. Apple told Reuters that its keyboard is designed to avoid potentially inappropriate or offensive suggestions, but iPhone users can still type in what they want, or override predictive text suggestions. “Apple did not immediately respond to The Verge’s request for comment.

Where To Upgrade Weapons In Phantom Blade Zero

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Phantom Blade Zero doesn't follow the RPG trend of having weapons in different tiers that you replace with newer ones. Instead, it lets you upgrade the weapons you're using to match the difficulty of the locations you're in. With this system, you can keep using the same weapon throughout the game if you'd like and just keep upgrading it to stay effective.

However, to upgrade these weapons, you'll need resources specific to the weapon type, similar to how you choose your skills. This means you can upgrade different weapon types, but because each type has finite resources, you need to choose which weapons go best together.

To upgrade your weapons in Phantom Blade Zero, you'll need to use the bells you use to upgrade abilities and fast travel around the map. At the bell, select Upgrade Equipment to open your inventory and choose what you want to improve. Hold the prompted button (X on PS5) to upgrade the weapon you choose.

From this menu, you can choose from your Main Weapons, Phantom Edges and Accessories, and they all get upgraded similarly. Choose the item you want to upgrade, and it will show you what resources you need. Although the resources differ, they all require Remnant Dust, just like upgrading your abilities.

For regular weapons, there are three different weapon types to choose from: One-Handed, Two-Handed and Dual-Wield. To upgrade these, you'll need Forged Metal, Twinned Metal and Broadplate Metal, respectively. You'll also need Solvent, which applies to any weapon you're upgrading.

However, as you level your weapons higher, you'll need to improve the materials, too. For example, for one-handed weapons, you can use Forged Metal to upgrade up to level 10, but you need Polished Forged Metal to upgrade from level 10 to level 20. Furthermore, you'll need Flawless Forged Metal to upgrade beyond level 20 to max level.

The same applies to the solvent you use to upgrade weapons. Solvent is used for upgrading weapons up to level 10, Concentrated Solvent gets you from level 10 to level 20 and Universal Solvent gets you the rest of the way to max level.

The Bashpole gives you one extra way to find hidden treasure.

Upgrading Phantom Edges functions the same as upgrading weapons, but requires different materials. Just like how weapons have three different types - One-Handed, Two-Handed and Dual-Wield - there are five different types of Phantom Edges: Martial, Destruction, Artifice, Ranged, and Summon.

The materials required for upgrading Phantom Edges are as follows.

Reverting weapons is one of the game's best features because it lets you freely try different weapons as you play. If you revert an upgraded weapon, it returns to level 1 and refunds all resources used to upgrade it. This means you aren't penalized for experimenting with different weapons.

However, this does get more complicated as you upgrade weapons to higher levels. For levels 1-10, reverting weapons is free, but for higher levels, you have to use a Reforge Talisman. Reforge Talismans are available for both weapons and Phantom Edges, and they are not refunded after you revert levels.

This means you need to be more thoughtful about which weapons you upgrade and revert once you reach higher levels. Luckily, by the end of the game, you'll already know which weapons work best for your play style, so you won't need to revert as much.

Upgrading Accessories is a similar process to upgrading weapons and Phantom Edges. Go to a bell and select Upgrade Equipment, then go to the Accessories tab at the top of the menu.

To upgrade Accessories, you need only one resource along with your Remnant Dust: Tainted Crystal Shards. You can find these around the world like the rest of the in-game resources, but you can not revert accessories like you can with weapons and Phantom Edges.

Once you use them, you can't get Tainted Crystal Shards back.

Soul Tempering is the highest level of upgrading that you can do in Phantom Blade Zero. To do so, you need to find materials throughout the game that are specific to each weapon or Phantom Edge. You unlock Soul Tempering only after you've spoken to Myst at the Order's tavern in Pangzhen.

To Soul Temper a weapon or Phantom Edge, you have to be in the upgrade menu. At the bottom left, a prompt will appear to open the Soul Tempering menu. When you press it, it pulls up the list of weapons or Phantom Edges you have and lets you Soul Temper them if you have the right resources.

You can find Soul Temper materials in various places, such as chests or by defeating bosses. You only need one of each material to Soul Temper a given item, and you can't revert the upgrade or do it more than once.

A Stranger Things prequel series is coming to Netflix in 2028

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Other Worlds: A Stranger Things Adventure takes place in Nevada in the 1950s.

It's Stranger Things day, and Netflix is marking the occasion by giving the go-ahead to a live-action prequel series. The company hasn't announced too many details about Other Worlds: A Stranger Things Adventure, though did confirm it takes place in the 1950s, before the events of the original show and the stage play Stranger Things: The First Shadow. The new series is coming to Netflix in 2028.

An announcement teaser reveals that Other Worlds is set in Marion, Nevada in 1955. It shows an abandoned bicycle with a radio attached before the camera pans up toward a "restricted area" sign then an ominous-looking sky that erupts with a mighty boom.

"We've been dreaming of Other Worlds: A Stranger Things Adventure for many years now, and to see it become a reality is surreal and exhilarating," Stranger Things creators Matt and Ross Duffer said. "It's Stranger Things, but also thrillingly fresh, with a new setting, new heroes and villains, and new ... actually, we're not allowed to say any more. Now, on to casting. In the meantime — watch the skies, nerds!"

Although they've left Netflix for Paramount, The Duffer Brothers are executive producers on Other Worlds. Kate Trefry — a longtime writer on Stranger Things who was behind the script of The First Shadow — is the co-creator, showrunner and writer of the new series. Joe Wright, known for his work on films such as Pride & Prejudice and Atonement, will direct the first trio of episodes.

Elsewhere in the world of Stranger Things, The First Shadow will wrap up its runs on London's West End on December 27 and Broadway on January 3 (a recording of the play is reportedly coming to Netflix). The second season of animated spinoff Stranger Things: Tales from '85 hit Netflix in September. The company also released a blooper reel from the final season of the main show.

Kenyan doomsday sect leader jailed for 200 years over 191 murders

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Paul Mackenzie convicted over the deaths of 190 children and one adult after followers were ordered to starve themselves and their families

The leader of a Kenyan doomsday sect has been sentenced to 200 years in prison for the murders of 190 children and one adult in one of the world’s worst cult disasters in recent history.

The sentence, comprising 100 years for murder by starvation and 100 years by direct homicide, came two weeks after the court in Kenya’s coastal city of Mombasa found the cult leader guilty of the crimes.

Prosecutors said Paul Mackenzie and his Good News International church ran ​a Christian doomsday sect whose members were ordered to starve themselves and their children to death to go to heaven before the world ended.

His accomplices threatened and beat victims to ensure they followed his orders. The emaciated bodies were found scattered in shallow graves and dumped in latrines. Some had been buried alive.

Mackenzie was arrested in 2023 after police received a tipoff, and weeks later authorities began uncovering bodies in shallow graves in the Shakahola forest near the coast in south-eastern Kenya.

In total, more than 400 bodies have been recovered. Many were severely decomposed and only 26 could be identified.

Mackenzie denied murder charges, saying he did not force the victims to starve, but the court rejected that argument.

The Shakahola tragedy has gripped Kenya since the discovery of mass graves in the forest more than three years ago.

It raised questions about how Mackenzie was able to continue operating despite being on the radar of the authorities since 2017, when police searched his premises.

In 2019, authorities shut down Mackenzie’s church and he relocated ‌to the Shakahola forest, police said.

The case has also led to calls for tougher oversight of churches and other religious organisations in a country where religious faith plays a prominent role in public life.

Mackenzie, a former taxi driver, forbade cult members from sending their children to school and from going to hospital when they were ill, describing such institutions as satanic.

Cult members lived in makeshift homes of polythene sheeting and thatch that Mackenzie divided into areas with biblical names such as Jerusalem and Judea.

The plight of his followers began ​to emerge in March 2023, when a man told ​police his brother and wife had starved their children ​to death in the forest on Mackenzie’s orders.

Mackenzie was arrested but released on bail, after which relatives said he brought forward the date ​on which he told his followers the ‌world would end. Police rearrested him and ​started combing through the forest more systematically and began exhuming mass graves.

Last month’s judgment covered 191 deaths while investigations into more than 200 other deaths are continuing.

Justice Kavedza-Mochache said in a hearing lasting more than eight hours that in some cases children were isolated and denied food while their parents watched.

Mackenzie faces other charges over the forest deaths, including those of terrorism, which he has denied.

Prosecutors said that while he was in detention he also directed the deaths of 52 people in Kwa Binzaro village, about 18 miles from Shakahola. Mackenzie has pleaded not guilty in that case.
